ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u4	Thank you. Dear Marshal! Minister! In Poland we have very rich traditions related to the aviation industry. We have facilities in the form of airlines in Mielec and Świdnik, but we also have about 100 plants in the Aviation Valley. Young Polish engineers from Rzeszów are working on the development of the design of unmanned cameras. There is great potential here, both in the civil sector and in military applications. Therefore, thanks to the use of modern technologies and intellectual facilities, Polish industry can dominate the drone industry in the long term. We also have an interesting offer on the manned aircraft market. Here you can quote, for example, a Skytruck transport plane with an interest worldwide or an agricultural Dromader plane. Developing these projects and building them on native technologies - this is important in my opinion - can be a huge opportunity for the whole industry. Poland has very strong academic centres and research institutes specialising in the aviation industry. We have first and foremost highly qualified staff and this is our big plus here and the advantage. What is the Ministry's action on the development of aviation companies? What specific programmes, such as EU funds, are targeted at the aviation industry? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u5	Thank you very much. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development, Minister Jadwiga Emilewicz, answers the questions. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, First of all, we thank you very much for this question, because indeed these threads, which were raised by Members in the question, concerning the development potential of the aviation industry, have been noticed in the Responsible Development Strategy in at least a few places, and in the most material way, as one of the flagship strategy programmes - in the program, which has a contractual name "Growd and Wigura." In your question there are two components, one concerning the development of the classic aviation industry, and the other concerning the development of unmanned aircraft. What we're aiming for, of course, is, given the base on which we're based... According to our estimates of how many companies operate in this area in Poland, it is about 130 companies, and directly in the sector there are more than 40 companies employing 20,000. employees. These are, on the one hand, the biggest global players who chose Poland because of not only labour costs, but above all, the engineering quality of the centres mentioned in the question and, on the other hand, it is already over 100 co-operatives in the Aviation Valley, small and medium-sized companies, but not only, because in other places in Poland this industry is also developing brilliantly. Thus, with such a basis, it seemed reasonable to include the industry in the Responsible Development Strategy. So what are the challenges and what are our expectations? Firstly, it is one of those industries which certainly have to respond to the challenges that are changing in the case of industrial transformation towards the so-called. Industry 4.0... Thus, the transition from a labour-intensive economy to a knowledge-based economy, achieving a balance in the labour market despite the shrinking resources, changing the positioning of Polish entrepreneurs in global value chains - and thus that the suppliers of these most complex engineering solutions for global players located in Poland are as much as possible - adapting the education system, because the human resource necessary for this sector is also extremely important, and we will be able to observe shortcomings in this area in the next few years, and finally coordinating legislation, especially when we are talking about the development of unmanned aircraft. So, what we are very strongly looking for is to support and strengthen competitiveness primarily in the transition from uncomplicated products to the lowest prices to the increasingly advanced ones. The goal we set ourselves is indeed to digitise as many processes as possible. This is the purpose of the legislation currently being prepared at the Ministry of Development, which aims to establish platform 4.0. Companies have already been invited to this platform, both the largest and the smaller subcontractors, in order to create a base, a good base for industry 4.0, for industry, where more and more workers will communicate not among themselves, but machines among themselves, in order to actually increase this production in Poland. The second element, among others. on this platform will take place, there will be even greater cooperation between science and industry, so that the cooperation of centres such as Warsaw University of Technology, Rzeszow University of Technology, Military Technical Academy, Mining and Hutnicz Academy or finally the Institute of Aviation in Warsaw with subcontractors, with entrepreneurs, will be even greater. It seems that it has actually been done as part of the work on the bill. In a material sense, this is the continuation of the INNOLOT program, a program prepared by the National Centre for Research and Development, whose first edition in the context of the previous financial perspective ended and caused that several excellent engineering projects were actually carried out. Now work is being done on changing the research agenda in cooperation with companies that operate in this area, and research centres, in order to launch another call for the next edition of INNOLOT this year. The budget of this programme will be no less than PLN 400 million, but it will be possible to increase these measures further under the agenda. In the second area, which Members have been asking about, namely the development of drones, we are in the process of developing, and by June we will be ready to prepare a legislative work plan in three areas. First, a catalogue of legislative changes necessary for the development of unmanned systems in Poland. Here we cooperate with the Civil Aviation Office, the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of National Defence on the necessary changes, which will allow a much larger number of services, both for the civil sector and for the harder one, to be provided in Poland. Moreover, we are also actively involved in the development of certain standards for the use of drones within the European Union, because we know very well that if this sector can be competitive, today, when it comes to years of unmanned operation, it is not just flying equipment, but all the software and visualisation is the greatest added value that we can bring to this sector, so we are also actively involved in standardising certain services within the European Union. This catalogue of legislative amendments will therefore be prepared by June. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Secondly, identification [[♪ The bell ♪]] the needs of the public administration. And thirdly, the center of advanced services provided by drones. These are things that will be developed by June - as well as a specially dedicated program at the National Centre for Research and Development. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u14	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, As regards the pension scheme of professional soldiers, the current management of the national defence department is not planning any changes in the pension system of professional soldiers in the active service. Once again, when asked by Members of the Civic Platform, we answer that no changes in the pension system of professional soldiers in the active service are planned. The proposal, which Mr Sejm is talking about on 2 December, to amend the Act on pension provision for professional soldiers and their families, which provides for the reduction of pensions, I note, of military pensions, concerns among others. members of the WRON, former professional soldiers who served in the totalitarian state: in the WSW, in the Management Board of the Second General Staff. This project, which lies in the Seym, is an expression of elementary social justice. It is not acceptable for soldiers who served the totalitarian state to receive much higher pensions than other servants in the military. The main objective of this project is to ensure that the situation when pensioners serving either in the Board of II General Staff, or in the ISS, receive pensions of more than 10,000. zł, when others receive much lower salaries... So that I stress once again, Mr President: this is an expression of elementary justice, so that such benefits are not received by people who are unworthy to receive such benefits in free Poland. I would like to stress once again that the Ministry of National Defence, and especially the Minister of National Defence, as well as the entire management of the Ministry, aims to ensure that the soldiers who are serving today receive a decent salary, a decent allowance and that they have stability and security of service. These priorities quoted by the Minister at the outset are fulfilled by the Ministry of National Defence, as an example of which is a project which will stand on today's Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers and which restores the right of professional soldiers to full coverage in the event of medical dismissal to 30 days. This will be paid 100% , and not - as so far - in a reduced height. I would also like, Minister, to remind you of what took place during the rule of the Civic Platform in 2010 and 2012 when the departure from military service concerned by far more soldiers, officers, petty officers than those 1.5 years of rule of law and justice. At that time, housing allowances of about PLN 400 million were paid. Nor can we agree with the allegations that soldiers are leaving the Polish Army or are being fired. According to data compiled by the Department of Personnel, 267 colonels left the army in 2016, for example, in 2010, when the PO-PSL coalition ruled, 294 colonels left. As for the estimates that are planned for the coming months or years, we are not able to make such estimates, as more than 80% of professional soldiers leave as a result of the termination of the service. Between 2010 and 2011, under the rule of the Civic Platform and PSL, the service was terminated by 531 colonels, and in 2015 and 2016 by 402. I would also like to draw attention to the amount of expenditure from the State budget related to housing checks. For example, in 2016, a total of 879 benefits of this type were paid. The figures indicated show that it is completely unfounded that politicians of the Civic Platform make an argument about mass departures and even purges in the army. Your marshal, thank you.

We say, for example, "Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, They call their cell phones and they know they have cell phones. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u27	Thank you very much. Deputy Attorney General Robert Hernand will answer the question.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u28	Dear Marshal! Dear Members, In answer to the question of the Member, I would like to inform you that the National Public Prosecutor's Office, having regard to the letter from the Office of Competition and Consumer Protection, already in 2016, decided to check how many such proceedings are registered at national level. 700 such proceedings have been disclosed, including decommitted proceedings or proceedings terminated by the initiation of a preliminary investigation. In view of the fact that a number of economic operators have been exposed, not only those whom you have spoken of, but also other economic operators who offer telecommunications services, but not so much telecommunications services, because also energy and medical services, the prosecution has decided to hand over to one prosecutor, the District Prosecutor's Office in Płock, all these procedures to check the personal links between these economic operators, because they have been disclosed, and this is, of course, the investigative version, that these entities are either set up by the same persons or are linked to each other. All these proceedings completed by remission and refusal to initiate proceedings will be examined by the District Attorney's Office in Płock, which is conducting a wide-ranging investigation in this case. The proceedings shall be conducted in the direction of Article. 286 Penal Code, that's fraud. The method of action, as the honourable Member has pointed out, is to use the ignorance of older people, not just older people, but, in a very simple and understandable way, to submit an extension offer, for example. the telecommunications service used by that person, and the effect of that is that the person simply signs a contract with the new operator, completely unaware of it. Personal data of these persons are also used. It is very often the case that the contract signed by this person is not left at the place of residence, only the person who offers the service will then inform that he will send the contract back. Contracts are also counterfeited using personal data and their signatures. In general, specific economic operators shall transfer powers and powers to other persons under the agency agreement. They are often natural persons who, in the context of agency agreements and powers of attorneys, contact the victims, such as the mechanism for committing the crime. As I said, these are not only ICT services, but also energy services or health services. Let me give you an example of one of the companies. The subject of the contract was the sale of a device for measuring heart rate parameters, an EKG, which, through a telephone connection to the helpline and then a doctor in this company, was to convey a message about the condition, the correctness of the heart function of the person holding the camera. As I have pointed out, this is the 700 cases that will be investigated in this particular prosecutor's office in order to establish a mechanism for committing a crime, establish personal links and identify all persons responsible for committing a crime. At present, 14 people have been charged in this particular procedure. This is, of course, the beginning of the procedure, because as I have pointed out, the remaining proceedings in this area will be examined. And so, in addition to my statement, the element of deception is that you are simply presenting to this person an alleged annex to the agreement which is in force and which the person has signed earlier. This is not an annex, it is a new agreement with a completely different content, very often these agreements are counterfeited. That's all I can say. Thank you very much. [[Thank you very much.]]

So it should be possible to take this information from a register where such data would be collected. The records of the Minister of Health are, of course, the basis for this. I think I'm sure it would prefer to include this element. But it's not that simple, unfortunately, that we're just going to type it in. On the other hand, I would like to come back because I did not have time to answer the question about mDocuments, which Mr Wozniak asked in the first part. The process of implementation of mDocuments is underway. He will be divided into several stages. It will consist primarily of the possibility of displaying document data on the access devices of the administration services, the same as on the documents. First of all, it will be an ID card, then a driver's license, registration and compulsory vehicle insurance document. This process should be implemented by the end of this year. The pilot should be implemented, probably in June, and within 2 months we should come to the High House with a bill that will enable it to do so. However, the next stages will already be based on applications and we will gradually add documents that will already be in a more advanced form, in the form of applications for smartphones. These will be very different documents, both mandatory, basic documents, such as identity cards, and this will be in the first place, as well as documents such as documents. Student ID. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u62	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Professor! In April 2016, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation has drawn up a document on the development plan for inland waterways in Poland for the years 2016 and 2020 with a view to 2030, which was adopted on 14 June 2016 by the Council of Ministers. This document sets out directions for the development of inland waterway transport in Poland. The consequences of the measures adopted by the Ministry were the ratification by President Andrzej Duda on 6 March 2017 of the AGN Agreement, namely the European Agreement on Central Inland Waterways on International Meaning. Poland, as a member of the AGN Agreement, undertakes to ensure on the waterways covered by the Agreement navigation conditions meeting the criteria applicable to inland waterways of international importance in the so-called. Class E, corresponding to at least class IV sailing. Currently, the Ministry is involved in preparing the tender documentation necessary for the development of a feasibility study for the Odrzaska Waterway. The Ministry's plans set out in the document "Assumptions to plans for the development of inland waterways in Poland' include: the achievement of an international class of navigability in the Oder, on the Odrzaska Waterway and its inclusion in the European Waterway Network, TEN-T. The first step in implementing this priority is the elimination of the so-called "defence." bottlenecks, i.e. places that restrict inland navigation. In some cases, narrow throats include infrastructure crossing inland waterways, including bridges whose clearance over the sailing route is too small. It should be stressed that the Minister responsible for water management is now responsible for maintaining inland waterways, including ensuring adequate clearances under bridges. It is now the Minister of the Environment. Currently, water administrations in cooperation with the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping carry out tasks concerning the reconstruction of five bridges on the Odrzaska Water Road. The reconstruction of the bridge indicated by Mr Krosno Odrzański is carried out as part of the task of: The Oder regulation in the section from the Lower Bank to the mouth of Nysa Łużycka in the scope of: Renovation and modernization of the regulatory structure of Oder flowing freely - reconstruction and modernization of the regulatory structure, in order to adapt the section of Oder from Malczyc to the mouth of Nysa Łużycka to the third class of the water road. The project is indicated in the flood protection project of the Oder and Vistula basin and the financial resources for the performance of the task come from, among others. from the Operational Programme of the Infrastructure and Environment of the 2014-2020 World Bank and the Development Bank of the Council of Europe. Currently chosen by the Regional Water Management Board in Wroclaw, the consultant develops the documentation necessary for the reconstruction of some bridges on Odra and Warta, including the bridge in Krosno Odrzański. The information obtained from the Regional Water Management Board shows that in the fourth quarter of 2019 the development of the necessary documentation for the implementation of this investment is to be completed, while according to the agreed timetable the reconstruction of the bridge should be completed by 2023. So much for the specifics. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u78	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer on behalf of the Minister and the Ministry of National Defence. I want to say that there are two questions that are practically forcing a repetition of the answers, so please excuse me if I leave something for the second answer or if I repeat some of the explanations at the moment. In the opinion of the Ministry and the persons conducting this tender, nothing undermines the integrity of the operation, i.e. I consider the question of the fairness of this tender to be abusive and I say that there are no grounds for making such suggestions at this point in time. On the other hand, I welcome the question of transparency to be clarified. Of course, this is necessary and important. I would therefore like to say that the date on which the proceedings were opened was the transmission of a contract notice to the Official Journal of the European Union for publication. Thus, the procedure was conducted in one of the most competitive modes provided for in the Public Procurement Law, i.e. in the negotiation procedure with an announcement. In the open to the whole aviation market - I stress this - the proceedings were submitted by four entities: the Glomex consortium with Lufthansa, Megmar Logistics, Boeing Company and Aerospace International Group with the Polish entity. In the course of the proceedings, other parties were asked questions, e.g. by Embraer, and the application for an extension of the time limit for the submission of tenders in the proceedings, already during the proceedings, was submitted by Airbus. The purchaser accepted this request and extended the time limit for the submission of tenders by almost 2 weeks. Following the subject-matter assessment, two entities were selected for the further stage of the proceeding: the Glomex consortium with Lufthansa and the Boeing Company. The other participants were excluded. One of them, a contractor, or consortium of Aerospace International Group together with a Polish entity, was regarded by the Military Counterintelligence Service as not reliable enough to exclude a threat to the security of the state. The same contractor appealed to the National Board of Appeal against this decision, which rejected the appeal and thus maintained the position of the Ministry. On the other hand, Megmar Logistics Consulting did not submit the required documents in the proceedings. So there are two left. The qualified entities were negotiated with the aim of discussing the terms of the agreement and, above all, of colliding the technical requirements of the contracting authority with the capabilities of the aviation market. The course of negotiations with contractors was very much similar to the submission of correct tenders by all entities qualified to participate in the proceedings, and the submission of defective offers by all participants constituted circumstances which the contracting authority did not have a basis for foreseeing. The contracting authority took into account the comments submitted by the contractors in the negotiation procedure and intended to conclude an appropriate agreement, provided that only in the proceeding were tenders submitted correctly. I would like to remind you that the procedure was extended as soon as it was opened. The proceeding was annulled as both lodged on 9 March this year. offers contained formal errors, i.e. the content of the tenders submitted did not correspond to the specifications of the essential terms of the contract. Therefore, conditional offers were submitted. In addition, one of the contractors did not submit the required deposit. Therefore, both offers were rejected. The contracting authority has therefore given due diligence to enable the parties involved in the proceedings to submit correct tenders, so the reason for the situation is not on the part of the Ministry, i.e. the contracting party. In addition, the position of the contractors presented during the negotiations and the comments we submitted and taken into account did not indicate that there was no possibility to submit correct offers. It seemed that everything would be filed in accordance with the regulations and requirements. The defects of these tenders submitted could not be foreseen by the contracting authority. The fact that the competitive procedure was annulled was only due to the fault of the economic operators who submitted the offers to be rejected. The purchaser made good efforts to enable the submission of important offers, and the negotiations conducted, including concessions on our part, i.e. from the contracting party, were significantly consistent with the possibility of submitting important offers, i.e. without legal defects. Failure to enter into an agreement as a result of competitive proceedings would threaten to disrupt the financing of the entire programme, the lack of delivery of aircraft within the scheduled deadlines, in fact a delay of several years, and inconsistencies in the measures already taken in the area of the entire VIP fleet recovery plan. It was the urgency of the award of the contract, not the technical specification of the aircraft B 737-800, that was the main condition for selecting the award procedure. At the same time, at the end of this speech, I would like to remind you that the whole procedure continued - assuming that we started it immediately after we took over - 16 months, not 96 months. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u96	Thank you very much. And I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u97	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your question, I would like to inform you that on 3 October 2015 the provisions implementing Regulation No 1308/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council entered into force in the Polish legal order. 2013 under which the obligation to conclude contracts for first delivery to the first buyer was introduced, inter alia. cereals, sugar, dried fodder, hop seeds, tobacco, products processed from milk, milk products, beef, pigmeat, mutton, goats, eggs, meat, etc., and therefore practically all products, agricultural raw materials. According to Art. Article 38q (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 shall apply. 2 of that law, where the first buyer is not the processor, the contract between subsequent purchasers shall also be concluded in writing. Thus, these provisions were introduced, adopted in July 2015. They entered into force on 3 October 2015, i.e. during the previous government and the previous coalition. The amendment, which entered into force in February this year, was adopted in December Ub. r., introduced non-contractual sanctions, because from the side of agricultural organisations too we have received requests, signals that contracts are still not concluded, farmers are still not sure that the raw material they produce will find the recipient at a price which was previously established between the parties under conditions that were previously established. I will admit that by introducing these provisions, we had not one signal that the rules adopted in 2015 went too far. There was no indication from farmers or agricultural organisations or processors or any member of the agri-food supply chain that the rules introduced in 2015 should be amended. That's why we didn't change those rules or commas, which were left in that version. Indeed, after the imposition of sanctions, there were indications that the rules were going too far. But I would like to assure you from this point of view that the Agricultural Market Agency under the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development will not punish one farmer, not one Polish entrepreneur, for not concluding a contract if they do not deceive their partner farmer if they do not apply unfair practices. Because that is what this law is meant to do to strengthen the position of the farmer to give the farmer the opportunity to plan production. This also strengthens the whole agri-food sector, because the end result is that the relationship between the participants in this chain is to be organized. So, of course, in view of these opinions, we are examining these provisions more deeply at the moment, and we are preparing changes that will clarify these rules, and they will not make it really difficult for farmers, especially, but also for all those in the chain, to function or, as the Member has said, to prevent the sale of small batches of products at all. But it is important to remember that it is mainly the smaller farmers who have problems with the lack of contracts, but that they are not sure that the agricultural raw materials they produce will find a buyer. The larger ones are generally linked to some customers of fixed contracts. It must be borne in mind that if an agreement has been concluded even earlier and meets the requirements of the Regulation of the European Parliament, it does not need to be re-consigned each time, there is no such obligation. In addition, we in the Act have recorded the possibility to specify the dates for concluding contracts. Such deadlines for the moment have not been imposed by the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development for individual agricultural raw materials, and therefore only one provision applies that the contract must be concluded before delivery. There's no time that has to go by. We are aware that for some raw materials this time will have to be determined in the end, because some crops have to be planned in advance for longer, especially industrial crops that cannot find too many customers on the market. So, in conclusion, I would like to say that these provisions will be clarified, and they will be amended to the extent that they have come to us from the comments that have come to us. I am also convinced that these regulations are needed by all parties to supply, participants in the agri-food chain and should operate in Poland. [[♪ The bell ♪]] to stabilise the agricultural production market, the agricultural raw materials market. Thank you.

Paragraph 83f. 1 points 3a and 3b authorising the cut-off on the private owner's land for non-economic purposes and for the restoration of agricultural use. As we read in the justification for this project, the changes made will give us the opportunity to verify whether the cut-off was actually carried out for non-economic purposes. The natural person who wants to cut the trees on his property will be obliged to report it to the competent municipal authority. The Office will, in turn, keep a register of notifications, examine the facts and draw up a minutes of inspections to be made available to the persons concerned. The Office may object within 14 days of the inspection. It may also, if there are no grounds for objection, issue a non-substantiation certificate before the expiry of that period. If no objection is raised, the owner may cut out the trees. On the other hand, if, within 5 years of the inspection, a decision on the construction permit is requested and the construction is related to the operation of the business and will be carried out on the part of the property on which the tree has grown, the competent authority shall impose an obligation to pay the appropriate removal fee. The draft contains provisions imposing an obligation on the Minister responsible for the environment to determine the rates of removal fees which depend on the type or species of tree or the circumference of the trunk. Until rates are fixed, maximum rates shall apply. The Act is to enter into force after 14 days, with the exception of the provisions giving legal basis for the adoption of regulations setting out the criteria for recognition as natural monuments and the rate of removal fees which enter into force six months after the date of publication. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u273	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To begin with, I would like to refer to the three projects that the Sejm has examined in the debate. With regard to the Modern Project, the Minister of the Environment negatively refers to the proposal to amend the Nature Conservation Act published in print No 1361, which provides for the introduction of a new form of nature conservation to be protected, the obligation to plant replacements in any situation and, above all, to restore to a large extent the obligation of natural persons to obtain permission to remove trees and shrubs. Regardless of the intentions of the authors of the project, it is difficult to positively assess the changes that hit the property of ordinary citizens, restoring permits for the removal of trees and shrubs. Mandatory replacement plantings in any case are unrealistic, as especially in large cities in the real estate in which investments are carried out, and nearby there may be no place to plant new trees and shrubs and their proper growth. The new form of nature protection proposed by the authors was, in turn, to be recognised against the will of the owner of the property, on which the green is located, which was to be covered by this form of protection, limiting the owner's right to own the land. Such a proposal cannot be accepted. This could also have financial consequences because, in accordance with Article. 129 Environmental law The owner of such a property could simply apply to the municipality for compensation. With regard to the Civic Platform project, it should be noted that the draft Law on the amendment of the Nature Conservation Act No 1317, drawn up by Members of the Civic Platform, is nothing more than an attempt to automatically restore the rules on the removal of trees or shrubs in force before 1 January, i.e. before the current rules enter into force. Such a proposal must at least surprise, given that it has already been mentioned here today, the position expressed during the work of the Parliament's Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry by the authors of this project, according to which the regulations in force before 1 January had a number of disadvantages and needed to be amended. By the way, the failure of previous solutions has also been widely recognised. In my view and in my opinion of the Ministry, the adoption of the proposed solutions would be a return to the wrong system of restrictions on property rights, a system based on orders and penalties, reporting to neighbours, and would de facto carry the burden of taking care of greens important from the point of view of the local community from the shoulders of local self-government, of which it is a duty, on the shoulders of ordinary people. The Ministry of Environment cannot accept the proposal for this change. As regards the draft Law and Justice, the Ministry of the Environment took note of the solutions proposed in the Parliamentary draft law amending the Nature Conservation Act, which is included in print No 1334 and which provides for the obligation for individuals to notify their intention to remove a tree or shrub, to grant the possibility of objecting to such removal, to pay fees for the removal of a tree or shrub if an application for a construction permit is submitted within 5 years for carrying out a business activity, and to provide for penalties for infringement of these new provisions. With regard to the above-mentioned proposal, I would first of all like to point out that, in the light of the Act of 16 December 2016, the exemption of natural persons from the obligation to submit to administrative procedures for the removal of trees and shrubs, imposing new obligations on these persons and introducing the possibility of imposing severe penalties for infringements is a step backwards. The regulations resulting from the last year's amendment, according to the Ministry, have put in place the right solutions to restore the right for citizens to manage the greenery on their property, eliminating unnecessary, costly administrative procedures and the possibility of imposing high, ruining penalties for unlicensed cuts, often amounting to tens or even hundreds of thousands of zlotys. Therefore, the environmental department critically assesses the reference to the proposed return to the restriction system. Introduced on 1 January this year. In particular, the changes revealed the weakness of the spatial planning system, the lack of inventory of greenery at municipal level, the poor control of local authorities over compliance with the Nature Conservation Act and other long-term negligence of local authorities in this respect. It should be stressed that matters relating to green areas and trees under the Municipal Government Act are the tasks of the municipalities themselves. [[Exactly.]] In our view, the focus of local authorities on the strict regulation of property owners' activities, which are covered by greenery, observed by the end of last year, meant a genuine restoration of the obligation to maintain greenery, a shift of these obligations to ordinary citizens. Therefore, the environmental resort does not agree with the direction of the changes proposed in this draft amendment to the Nature Conservation Act. The proper state of the local green should, in our view, result not from new orders, restrictions and penalties, but from strict enforcement of local government obligations or the introduction of additional financial instruments to encourage ordinary citizens to maintain greenery on their sites. It would be worthwhile to give the local government the power to conclude agreements with the owners of valuable trees and shrubs, under which the local government will finance their maintenance. Municipalities could also have the power to introduce a real estate tax relief - the greater the more valuable greenery there is on the plot. According to the Ministry of Environment, further work on amending the Nature Conservation Act No 1334 should go in this direction. As for the questions that were addressed directly to me, some of the questions were repeated. The most frequently repeated question was the question about the effects of this law, whether as Ministry we have estimated the number of clippings since 1 January. Yesterday, on the environmental committee, I presented this report. I also explained why this report did not even contain an estimated number of trees removed without authorisation in accordance with applicable law from private areas, because the authorities of the municipality, and we came here to four cities, did not send us such information. Krakow only described this situation very clearly. This is the task of the municipality itself to observe what is happening in its territory and possibly respond to any violation of the law. With regard to this section in Łeba, which we talked about, there was clearly a violation of the law by the owner and, therefore, certain sanctions should apply to him. As far as these data are concerned, I will only say briefly about Warsaw: on the basis of decisions issued last year and in the first two months of this year in Warsaw 5 thousand were removed. trees. As regards Mrs Lenartowicz's question regarding the prohibition of entry into the Białowieża Forest. Madame, the situation is this way. This prohibition was introduced in accordance with the Law on Forests by the State Forests, by the Regional Director and foreman from this area. The ban applies to this part of the Białowieża Forest in which no work is carried out, no protective cuts. Nor is it removed from this area - that is about 17 thousand. ha - trees threatening. You just don't do anything there. Forests are responsible for security in their territory, and therefore there is a ban. [[But why?]] However, this prohibition does not apply to hiking trails and all places for recreation, because the stand along both hiking trails and recreation sites, for example the natural path of the bison is led to such a state that it does not endanger the safety of the tourists there. [[All of a sudden it's getting dangerous?]] This is precisely because in this part of the wilderness, Madame, there are no cuts, no words, no cuts... ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u297	High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my views on the draft laws amending the Nature Conservation Act and the reports on these projects submitted by the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, prints No 1334, 1337, 1317, 1335, 1361 and 1411. Let us recall that the parliamentary initiatives respond to the media criticism of the solutions introduced by the Law of 16 December 2016. Still, despite extensive discussion, it is difficult to determine the exact scale of the tree clipping phenomenon after the entry into force of these provisions. With regard to the anti-Citizen's initiative of PO Members, print No 1317, we share the view of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry to reject this project at first reading, print No 1335. It should be noted that the radical tightening of existing rules for cutting trees and shrubs is a fatal solution as it limits the freedom of citizens. We believe that individual tree clippings are the nature of local landscape changes and do not significantly affect the state of the environment. The Modern Members' Initiative, print No 1361, should be assessed as an attempt to balance the relationship between the applicable rules and the overly restrictive provisions in force before 1 January 2017. However, this initiative is very inconsistent and re-introduction of the authorisation institution, which limits the rights and freedoms of citizens in a fundamental way. The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle agrees with the recommendation of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry to reject this project at first reading, print No 1411. We maintain the view that green areas in urban areas play an important role, affect people's well-being and shape a harmonised urban space that positively influences creativity and increases people's motivation to act. The quality of life in the city should be shaped primarily by spatial planning, which is the responsibility of local self-government units. Strengthening this instrument should be an inspiration for future legislative work. The Congress of Urban Movements brought very valuable comments during the discussion on nature conservation. They should be taken into account in the monitoring phase of current solutions and work on future changes in nature protection law. However, we must strongly emphasise that we see the fundamental difference between the general ban system and the granting of administrative permits for tree cutting and the protection of natural and landscaped areas. We are in favour of this second solution. During the committee's work a number of interpretative doubts were raised concerning the provisions proposed in the third initiative, PiS Members, print No 1334. It provides that the property on which the trees or shrubs have been removed may not be used for business activities for a period of 5 years. This provision seems to have a negative impact on the economic development of the country. If, for example, the owner of the property, where the family house is located with a garden, removes the tree, and in 2 years' time he will want to open a one-man business activity of an office character, then by registering the activity at the address of the place of residence, he will commit a delict as defined in the Nature Conservation Act or be forced to rent an office at the start to raise the costs of his business. This provision of the draft law from print No 1334 is completely irrational, in advance limiting the freedom to conduct business. Perhaps the applicants were more concerned with banning the development activity on the site where the logging was carried out, rather than the economic activity as such at all, but such a provision was not included in the draft law. Regardless of the type of activity carried out, the proposed solution raises considerable doubts as to its compatibility with the Constitution. There is also a high likelihood that there will be legitimate ways of avoiding the proposed ban, which could undermine the sense and practice of lawmaking. In addition, administrative fines for failure to notify the authority of the removal of the tree or shrub seem too severe. The socio-economic impact of the project is of fundamental doubt. It is also impossible to ignore the negative opinion of the Polish County Association, which has highlighted the numerous shortcomings of the proposed legislative solutions. This should lead, above all, to reflection on the mode of legislative work that is too chaotic in our opinion. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will abstain from voting on the draft Act amending the Nature Conservation Act 1334 and vote against the draft laws contained in the prints 1317 and 1361.

During the meeting with the repatriates on the 80th anniversary of the first mass deportation of Poles to Kazakhstan by the Soviet authorities, the president assured that the government would not rest until every Pole and every Polish who are abroad will return to Poland. The problems identified by the repatriates concerned cumbersome, unclear procedures related to the return, concerns about the future destination, concerns about the workplace, as well as concerns about poor knowledge of the Polish language. It is necessary to emphasize the fact that in this law we are resigning from the very often used, derogatory examination of Polish language. The new forms, the institutions that we are introducing, which will be introduced by this law, are government repatriating agents and adaptation centres for repatriates. An important issue, which was highly appreciated by both the repatriates and the repatriates themselves, was among others. the issue of financial assistance and the precise definition of conditions for settling. We hope that the newly introduced regulations will change the state, which has been since 2007, which in fact reduces the number of people who want to repatriate to our country, because the problem of repatriates is a problem that connects us all, all Poles in fact all over the world: both in the United States and in Kazakhstan. This differs significantly from the previous solution, which only provided for a grant for the municipality to adapt the apartments, to renovate or grant the apartment, but the money was taken by the municipality, not by the repatriates, so even though the municipality took the money and allocated the apartment, the repatriates were forced to pay the rent. Hence, among others. the flaws of this solution. Here we harmonise this solution, whether it's a nameless invitation, nameless invitation or a family path or an adaptation measure, this housing aid amounts to 25,000. and she is up to all elements, both for rent or in the form of a subsidy to purchase. So this is the amount for repatriates, not for municipalities. This is the whole change, a very important change in the philosophy of the approach to repatriation. The second change, which also involved many questions, is the change in which we include spouses in the definition of repatriate. So far one of the very important barriers was that even if the person was Polish and showed it, and the spouse was not Polish, he was not eligible for any help, so he was treated in Poland as a foreigner. Thus, he did not have automatically granted Polish citizenship, no aid was granted to him, even if he was retired. So far it worked so that even if there were two pensioners, but one of them was Polish and the other was not, then two pensioners came and the person of Polish origin received a pension, and the person of another nationality did not receive this pension, and of course with most countries, among others. with Kazakhstan, where there are the largest number of repatriates, we do not have agreements on the transfer of pension benefits, so in fact the spouse was deprived of the means of life. And people of course participate in social life, children go to school, can take up work, etc., so this adaptation is done in such an automatic and fluid way, hence the help of 200 zlotys is slightly greater than for others due to this openness, shopping or other matters. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u357	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I wanted to propose a bill to amend the Maritime Fisheries Act. The proposed law amends the Maritime Fisheries Act of 19 December 2014, in particular by repealing the provisions on aggregate fishing quotas, amending the rules on the transfer of individual fishing quotas or fishing days, their resignation and international exchanges. The draft law eliminates the maximum fishing capacity in the fleet segments referred to in Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1.3 Maritime Fisheries Act, including fishing vessels using which commercial fishing is carried out in those areas. In addition, the vessel owner may withdraw from all or part of the vessel in a special fishing permit the individual fishing quota of the species of marine organisms or fishing days concerned by 15 July. The amendments shall also consist of the repeal of Article. 89 Maritime Fisheries Act, which prohibited, with the exceptions provided for, the placing on the market of marine organisms derived from fishing carried out on the basis of permits, inter alia. within the framework of scientific research, and in addition to the content of Article. 127 Maritime Fisheries Act, paragraph 5, which will allow effective protection of areas excluded from commercial fishing. In addition, the draft law provides for the repeal of the article. 23 and Article 23 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 35 of the Law of 5 December 2008 on the organisation of the fish market due to the lack of EU regulation which would require such far-reaching The most important element of the draft law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act is the abolition of the possibility to transfer individual fishing quotas between fishing vessels. The rules currently in force allowing the transfer of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels put in practice a privileged position for shipowners who are able to pay a certain amount of money to the vessel owner from whom the above mentioned. the fishing quota is to be obtained. The main purpose of the allocation of fishing opportunities shall be to carry out fisheries understood as fishing for marine organisms granted in a special fishing permit for commercial purposes. The transfer of individual fishing quotas between shipowners should therefore only take place in exceptional cases. Individual fishing quotas or fishing days which shipowners are not able to use in accordance with the rules in force should be returned to the Minister responsible for fisheries, who, using the powers conferred by the legislator, would re-distribute those additional fishing quotas. The individual fishing quota or number of fishing days specified in the special fishing permit may be transferred in whole or in part to another fishing vessel of the same vessel which, for at least 24 consecutive months before the date of submission of the request for transfer, was the vessel owner of the fishing vessels involved in the transfer. This proposal provides for the abolition of the possibility of aggregate fishing quotas, as requested by the fishing environment. The solution for the creation of the aggregate fishing quota has not yet been used and there is also no information from the fishing environment that would allow for the conclusion that this regulation will be used in the future. The proposed law extends the period of resignation of the vessel owner in the calendar year concerned from all or part of the quota allocated to him in the special fishing permit of the individual fishing quota of the species of marine organisms or fishing days until 15 July of the year concerned. The proposed law repeals paragraph. Article 2 and Article 3 9 the Law of 19 December 2014 on Maritime Fisheries in view of the need to extend the scope of freedom of economic activity partly limited by them. The restriction on freedom of establishment is that new fishing vessels cannot be introduced in fleet segments where the fishing capacity established by these provisions has been exhausted. In addition, the proposal provides for amendments to the Article. 12 Maritime Fisheries Act by extending the catalogue of prohibited acts. It is designed to prohibit fishing for species of marine organisms whose common quota has been exhausted. By-catch of species of marine organisms identified under Article 1 shall also be prohibited. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 3th Maritime Fisheries Act has been exhausted. These provisions aim to ensure effective conservation of marine living resources. It shall also be designed that the Minister responsible for fisheries shall immediately be informed of the use of 90% in the calendar year concerned. the common fishing quota of the species of marine organisms concerned, the exhaustion in a given calendar year of the common fishing quota of the species of marine organisms concerned or the exhaustion in a given calendar year of the fishing quota of the species of marine organisms identified pursuant to Article. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Maritime Fisheries Act published this information on the website of the office serving it and in a customary manner adopted in the area concerned. The proposed law provides for the repeal of the Article. 89 Maritime Fisheries Act. The current rules prohibited, with the exceptions provided for, the placing on the market of marine organisms derived from fishing carried out on the basis of permits, inter alia. as part of scientific research. Fish caught in connection with scientific research or development or for the purpose of training, in the field of marine fisheries, had to be disposed of so far, which did not allow the use of marine organisms caught in another way, allowing them to be used properly. The restriction does not have an equivalent in European Union law. In the Law of 5 December 2008 on the organisation of the fish market, the proposed regulation provides for the repeal of Article. 23 and Article 23 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 35 and consequently also repeal point 7 of paragraph 7. A key argument for repealing the article. The 23th Law on the Organisation of the Fish Market, which requires the first sale of certain fish species only at the centre of the first sale, is the fact that EU law does not require such a tightening solution as has been adopted in this provision. On the other hand, according to the article. 35 Law on the organisation of the fish market recognised fish producer organisations are obliged to report data on first sale using electronic software to register sales and take over fish products. The system currently used by fish producer organisations to report first sale fulfils all the conditions of Regulation 1224/2009 and it is therefore not justified to maintain Article. 35 Law on the organisation of the fish market. The proposed law provides that cases relating to the transfer of individual fishing quotas or fishing days and the exchange of international fishing quotas on request, initiated and not completed by a final decision, shall apply until the date of entry into force of this Act. The most important change in the proposed project is the abolition of the possibility to transfer individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. The purpose of the proposed regulation is to create a barrier which will prevent the sale of individual fishing quotas and, in the assessment of part of the fishing environment, the sale of quotas granted in special fishing permits leads to the preference of shipowners who are able to pay a certain amount of money to the vessel from which the individual fishing quota is to be obtained. Under the current rules, the operator of the fishing vessel shall be entitled to renounce all or part of the individual fishing quota of the species concerned by 30 June. The extension of the deadline is intended to allow shipowners to better estimate the individual fishing quotas at their disposal and their own fishing opportunities so that the resignation concerns quotas which the shipowners will not be able to catch. By-catch of species of marine organisms whose quota allocated under the Maritime Fisheries Act will be exhausted will also be prohibited. The aim of this solution is to achieve more effective protection of marine living resources. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u368	I am so sorry, Member. We need to keep an eye on certain standards. Mr Tadeusz Azievicz, Civic Platform, has the floor. [[Member Dorota Arciszewska-Mielewczyk: Of course, one sentence would save your marshal.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u369	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act, print No 1404. High Seym! As already mentioned, the main objective of this project is to eliminate the negative phenomenon of the transfer of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. I would like to remind you that the total fishing quota allocated to the Republic of Poland in accordance with European Union rules is divided into individual fishing quotas which limit the size of fishing carried out by shipowners of fishing vessels. In the current legal state, shipowners of vessels who are unable to fish for an individual quota allocated to them for a given year shall have the right to transfer it to other shipowners with greater fishing opportunities. This is intended to make full use of the total amount allocated to Poland. This solution seems rational and is applied in other European Union countries, but according to some fishing communities it has negative consequences in terms of fishing quota trade. The result is the creation of a group of shipowners whose main source of income is not from fishing but from the sale of quotas allocated to them. On the other hand, shipowners who do not have adequate financial capacity cannot obtain an additional fishing quota, which deprives them of an opportunity to increase the profitability of their activities. The project's authors propose a far-reaching reduction in the possibility of transferring fishing quotas. This will only be possible between vessels belonging to the same ship owner. The remaining amounts are to be returned to the Minister responsible for fisheries, who will distribute them again. The proposal also provides for the abolition of the possibility of aggregate fishing quotas. This solution has not yet been used and there is no information from the fishing community on the interest in this form of business activity. The project also extends the catalogue of prohibited acts, e.g. a prohibition on fishing for species of marine organisms the common quota of which has been exhausted, which is intended to lead to more effective conservation of marine resources. Therefore, the authors propose that the Minister responsible for Fisheries should publish this information on the Authority's website as soon as information is provided on the use of the common quota of the species concerned in a given calendar year. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u371	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I present the position of the Poselski Kukiz Club15 on the government proposal for a law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act. After consulting the fish processors industry, I can say that the proposed law takes into account the interests of Polish fisheries operators in general. A good solution is to liquidate the monopoly of first-sales centres. Although, according to the control of the Supreme Audit Chamber, these centres were fully prepared to provide the services necessary for the effective introduction of fish for the first sale, ensuring that fish can be picked up and stored under appropriate conditions, the existing first sale rules did not favour the concentration of supply and the strengthening of the market position of the producer organisation. The choice by the producer of the place of sale and the buyer prevented the centres from carrying out their basic statutory task - auction or session sales of fishery products. The insufficient participation of the centres in the fish market has made their impact on improving the competitiveness and sustainability of the fishing sector small. The small efficiency of the centres, and thus their minor impact on improving the competitiveness of the fishing sector, was primarily due to the choice of the producer of the place of sale and the buyer. This allows fish producers to sell fish in any place they choose without any restrictions. However, NIK pointed out that for this reason most producers sold fish outside the centres, including directly to consumers in Denmark and Sweden, with the exception of the first sale of fish species whose resources require increased protection or surveillance, e.g. cod or salmon. Another reason for malfunctioning was the freedom of location of centres resulting in too many centres in one port; e.g. There were five centres in the port of Kołobrzeg managed by competing producer organisations. The small share of fish sales of centres in relation to their potential was also a consequence of the approach of controlled producer organisations which did not enforce from their members - or did not establish it at all - an obligation to sell all the catch through their own centres. Another change that will enter into force thanks to this Act will be the elimination of the negative phenomenon of trade in individual fishing quotas between shipowners. The changes were requested by some of the fishing communities concerned with the development of the maritime fisheries sector and the real pursuit of fishing activities. Under the rules in force, shipowners of fishing vessels who are unable to catch an individual fishing quota allocated to them for a given year may transfer it to another vessel which catches it, which should allow the use of the overall fishing quota allocated to our country. This solution was intended to make more efficient use of fishing quotas, but in practice it has led to individual trade in quotas between shipowners. The good news is that by changing the bill, there's going to be an order here. It is good that an extension of the period until 15 July for the vessel owner to submit an application for resignation in a given year is provided for in part or in whole of the individual fishing quota or fishing days allocated to it. To date, this application had to be submitted by 30 June. By extending this period, individual fishing quotas or fishing days available to shipowners can be better estimated so that the resignation concerns only the part which the shipowners will not be able to use until the end of the year. After consulting industry representatives, I can say with all responsibility that the law is the answer to the existing paradoxes that existed in the industry. He is pleased that the Polish government finally listened to the voice of fishermen on our coast. I would also like to add that in our consultations actually for 15 years there has always been this dissonance between Warsaw and Pomerania. The fishermen from Władysławów, Darlov, Świnoujście were not well listened to not only in Brussels, but also in Warsaw. So that we personally are very pleased with these changes to the bill and we are glad to continue to process them in the committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The proposed law amends the Law of 19 December 2014 on Maritime Fisheries, in particular by: repealing the rules on collective fishing quotas; amending the rules on the transfer of individual fishing quotas or fishing days; amending the provisions on the waiver of individual fishing quotas; departing from the limits of the maximum fishing capacity in the three main segments of the fleet, including the fishing vessels using commercial fishing in those areas; extending by 15 July the time limit for the vessel to resign from all or part of the quota granted to it in the special fishing permit of the individual fishing quota of the species of marine organisms or fishing days concerned; repealing Article. 89 Maritime Fisheries Act, which in principle prohibited the placing on the market of marine organisms derived from fishing carried out on the basis of permits, inter alia. within the framework of scientific research or development; addition to Article. 127 Maritime Fisheries Act, paragraph 5, which bans fishing in areas excluded from commercial fishing; repeal of Article. 23 and Article 23 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 35 of the Law of 5 December 2008 on the organisation of the fish market due to the lack of mandatory EU regulation which would require the use of restrictions of as wide scope as today. The main objective of the amendment is to eliminate trade in fishing quotas by the fishing environment, which in practice has led to an uneven and unfair distribution of fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels, with particular prejudice to smaller shipowners. The proposed changes in principle exclude this trade in fishing quotas, leaving only the possibility to transfer these quotas between individual vessels of the same ship owner. In order to avoid undue manipulation in this respect, a ship owner wishing to transfer all or part of the quota will have to demonstrate that he was a ship owner of fishing vessels involved in the transfer for at least 24 months prior to the transfer. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u389	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Law on Maritime Fisheries. The draft law repeals, among others, rules on the transfer of individual fishing quotas or fishing days between different shipowners. According to the existing provisions of the Act, it was possible to transfer individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. This solution was controversial and the sale of fishing quotas to certain shipowners was a substitute for the profits from fishing. The amendment of the law explicitly and in my view rightly excludes the possibility of transferring fishing quotas. The amendment shall only maintain the possibility for the vessel owner concerned to transfer fishing quotas between his vessels. This solution, it seems, is a logical procedure and beneficial for the interests of the state. The individual fishing quota or number of fishing days specified in the special fishing permit may be transferred in full or in part to another fishing vessel of the same vessel which, for at least 24 consecutive months before the date of submission of the request for transfer, has been the vessel owner of the fishing vessels involved in that transfer. This provision is intended to prevent temporary exchanges of fishing quotas between shipowners who, in order to make use of the economic situation, will temporarily re-subscribe their vessel to another vessel owner. The current rules allowing the transfer of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels in practice put ship owners in a privileged position who have large and free funds to buy out additional fishing quotas from shipowners who, for various reasons, did not want or could not use their allocation. I don't think that was fair. Unused amounts should be distributed fairly to other shipowners. Following the repeal of the provisions on the transfer of individual fishing quotas or fishing days between different shipowners in the Act, it was necessary to organise the whole of the Act by repealing or clarifying additional points relating to the repealed provisions. However, this does not affect the provisions of the Act and as an editorial procedure does not require justification at this stage. The amendment of the law in accordance with the suggestions of the fishing environment also abolishes aggregate fishing quotas. I am convinced that repealing the rules in this respect will not have a negative impact on fisheries. The Act also extends the period for the vessel owner to resign from all or part of the fishing authorisation granted to it in a specific fishing authorisation for the individual fishing quota of the species of marine organisms or fishing days concerned in the calendar year concerned. The extension of this deadline will make it easier for shipowners to assess their needs in this respect. A very important amendment provided for in the draft law is the repeal of the provisions which prohibited the placing on the market of marine organisms derived from fishing carried out on the basis of permits, inter alia. as part of scientific research. To date, fish caught to carry out research, development or training of marine fisheries specialists have been disposed of. The contested law allows the use of marine organisms caught by other means. I am convinced that the proposed law will contribute to the development of the marine fisheries sector and will put an end to the bad practices of ship owners so far. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni Circle asks for further proceedings of the law.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u391	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to you a draft law amending the Regional Accounting Chambers Act and certain other laws. The changes proposed in the draft law concerning the functioning and organisation of regional financial chambers should contribute to the effectiveness of the supervisory and control activities of the chambers. The launch of this draft law was due to the need to address the negative trends related to the over-indebtedness of local government units, which adversely affects the economic condition of the local government and the state. There are currently no fully adequate control mechanisms available for rapid diagnosis of the situation and potential risks to public finances. The control powers so far do not fully cover the financial activity of the local government, thus creating a legal gap that is used to the detriment of the finances of the local government. Here, as an example, the dangerous debt of Ostrowice in the last years in the West Pomeranian Voivodeship can be used - its debt has been over 400% in recent years. the amount of the annual budget. It is also necessary to improve the organisation of regional financial chambers, including inter alia. introducing an impartial procedure for the selection of persons in charge of chambers. The project presented to the State includes changes both to improve the control activities of the regional financial chambers and to optimise the achievement of the objectives of the control and supervisory activities of the chambers. In this respect, we extend the scope of the control activities of the Chambers by involving legal persons to which local government units, metropolitan unions and associations of local self-government units, associations of local self-government units and other local self-government units, including local self-government entities, directly or indirectly influence them. Further, we are creating an instrument enabling the President of the Chamber to inform the competent authority supervising the audited entity of the fact that the audited entity has failed to notify the performance of post-control applications or of unjustified non-execution of them - this is necessary in particular in situations of repeated persistent non-execution of post-control applications. In addition, the amendment provides for the supervisory procedure to include resolutions amending the budgetary resolution - which have not yet been included - and resolutions on the multiannual financial forecast or its amendment. It is also important to introduce an obligation for regional audit chambers to notify law enforcement authorities of a legitimate suspicion of the offence. The project also includes changes concerning the organisation of regional accounting chambers, moving towards higher standards for the work of chambers while opening up more access to work in chambers. Here we are introducing changes concerning the appointment of competition committees for the positions of Presidents of Chambers - these committees will be appointed at the President of the Council of Ministers, and not, as yet, within the Regional Audit Chamber. The competition committee will consist of 5 members: one representative of the President of the Council of Ministers, the Minister responsible for public administration, the Minister responsible for finance, as well as the National Council of Regional Courts of Auditors and the local government of the Joint Government and the Local Government. This will allow for impartial, non-intelligence and interdependencies within the Chamber to select the best candidates for presidents. At the same time, we allow you to apply for the status of a member of the college not only for a person with a 4-year traineeship in units connected with the functioning of local government or public finances, but also with 4-year scientific and teaching experience. The draft also includes proposals to seal anti-corruption bans by prohibiting the appointment of members of the college of persons who are punished by the prohibition on the exercise of functions relating to the disposal of public funds, as well as the performance of the post of non-tertiary member of the college by the person employed in that college. In addition, the amendment provides for the immediate enforceability of the decision to revoke the President, Deputy or Member when he has been convicted by a final judgment for a criminal offence committed by intent or has been legally punishable by a ban on public resources and the possibility for the President of the Council of Ministers to grant immediate enforcement of a decision to revoke the President, Deputy or Member where there is a risk of non-executing tasks in the Regional Audit Chamber or in the event of repeated violations of the law in the performance of his duties or of repealing them. This draft contains a number of provisions clarifying the Regional Financial Chambers Act. Here the example can be used, among others. identification of the tasks of the National Council of Regional Courts of Auditors, punitiveisation of certain behaviours related to the control carried out, e.g. failure to inform the performance of the post-control requests or to close the gap by ensuring that the Deputy President is directing the operation of the Chamber in the event of the death or immediate dismissal of the President. In addition, the project also provides for amendments to the Local Government Act, the Regional Government Act and the Regional Government Act, according to which, under certain conditions, the President of the Council of Ministers will be able to grant immediate enforcement of supervisory decisions concerning, first, the cancellation or termination of an executive body of a local government unit and the designation of a person who, until the new authority is elected, will be able to act as such - in the event of repeated violations of the Constitution or laws and the failure of the voivode to call on these authorities to cease infringements. Another element will be the suspension of the authority of local government units and the establishment of a committee board for a period of up to 2 years in the event of a rapid improvement and a prolonged lack of effectiveness in the performance of public tasks by the authorities of local government units. The project was subject to extensive consultation. As part of this consultation, we have seen in the majority acceptance of the solutions adopted in it. They were agreed and the project was also agreed within the Joint Government and Territorial Government Commission. The local government, the committee has given a positive opinion on this matter. It also requires that the draft law amending the Act on Regional Financial Chambers and certain other laws at the last meeting of the Joint Government and Local Government Commission was cited by the local government as a positive example of dialogue with the government. Thank you very much.
