In particular, 16 assumes that the contest committee for the President of the Chamber will be appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers. This solution will eliminate the potential risk of both the Presidents of the Chambers and the individual members of the college having an impact on the performance of the competition for the post of President in the next term of office. The amendment also introduces amendments to the appeal of both the President of the Chamber and his deputy in the event of a flagrant failure to comply with his duties. Additional mechanisms shall be put in place to ensure immediate response in situations where the chamber is dysfunctionally affected. Therefore, the decision to revoke the President of the Chamber will be entitled to immediate enforcement by the President of the Council of Ministers insofar as there is a risk of failure to perform tasks in the Chamber or where the reason for the appeal was a repeated violation of the law in the performance of the duties of the officer concerned. Seventhly, it is also necessary to seal the anti-corruption ban on civil law work for the entities referred to in Article. 1 paragraph 2, prohibiting such work as part of an additional employment relationship. The project, by sealing and strengthening the supervision of the financial economy of local government units, should have positive social and economic effects and ensure the smoother functioning of regional financial chambers. The Law and Justice Club will support the proposed amendments to the Act and recommend them to the High House. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u394	Thank you very much. Mr Jacek Protas, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u395	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I present an opinion on the Act amending the Act on Regional Financial Chambers and other laws. The proposed law is an attempt to transform an independent and objective control body into a subordinate government body with powers and tools to repress and discipline local government units according to unclear and vague criteria. As regards the justification for the project, we believe that there is no link between the state of over-indebtedness of a few small municipalities and the effectiveness of the financial control and supervision tools and mechanisms set out in the present Regional Audit Chambers Act. Rules to prevent over-indebtedness of local government units are enshrined in the Public Finance Act. [[Of course.]] They are rigorous enough to be properly enforced, which has been done so far. The mechanism adopted in this amendment is simple and clear, but I do not hesitate to say that it is also primitive: firstly, the submission of the regional accounting chambers of government administration, first of all by selecting and dismissing the President of these chambers, secondly, a significant increase in the powers of regional chambers by extending the scope of control and control functions - I would like to draw attention to two issues: access to databases and personal data of employees in controlled entities and the recording ordering controlled officials to transfer copies, copies or extracts of controllers without the head of the unit - thirdly, to equip the President of the Council of Ministers with the power to revoke, terminate or suspend each government body with an immediate declaration of enforceability. These changes in the law on municipal, district and voivodship authorities agree to the constitutionally guaranteed in the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the implementation of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) [[Applause]] Moreover, the vague and judgmental grounds for such action create uncontrollable freedom to abuse these drastic steps. In conclusion, the draft law does not result from the need to improve the effectiveness of the regional financial chambers, but rather from the desire to take away the subjectivity and independence of the local government units, gives the Prime Minister the unjustified power to reprisal wojites, mayors, presidents, district and voivodeships and their resolution bodies, including their cancellation or resolution. The amendment was adopted by Polish local governments, Minister, with concern and opposition. I would remind you that the record was that the agreement with the Joint Committee of the Government and the Territorial Self-Government was positively positive. I don't know what that means. It also raises huge doubts about the National Council of Regional Courts of Auditors that, I quote: the project submitted for consultation is inconsistent in many places and the proposed regulations conflict with the principles of the rule of law resulting from the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. And the second quote: Doubts arise as to whether the main objective defined by the designers, i.e. strengthening the safeguards against uncontrolled debt of local government units will be achieved after the proposed changes have been made. The project proposes a number of changes concerning the functioning and organisation of regional financial chambers. The most important of these are the subject of the supervisory procedure of resolutions amending the budgetary resolution, as well as resolutions on the multiannual financial forecast or its amendment, the appointment of competition committees to hold competitions for the Presidents of the Chambers at the President of the Council of Ministers, and not, as yet, within the Regional Financial Chambers, the possibility for the President of the Council of Ministers to give immediate effect to the decision to revoke the President of the Chamber - similarly in the case of the establishment of a committee board and the termination or revocation of the executive body of the local government - or, finally, the determination of the seat and territorial scope of the chambers, the local seats, the number of members of the formation college, the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister of Administration without the opinion of the Provincial Sejmika. High Izbo! The whole project dominates, and even strengthens, the function of control of individuals and its absolute consequences, including the issuing of decisions under immediate feasibility. In the justification of the project dominates, repeats such a basic idea of unfavorable or even hostile attitude towards local governments. It is local governments - according to the rulers - who are subject to the tendency of over-indebtedness, non-respect of post-control recommendations or not to do them at all, or even to combination. On many occasions local governments move to companies tasks that they do not want to be carried out by an executive body because of legal or ethical doubts. This is how the government justifies this project. And such a project, where only control and sanctions for local governments dominates, is unacceptable in our view. The project is a typical example of the next stage of self-government ownership and centralization of institutions. In fact, it seems unbelievable that the determination of the territorial scope of the Chamber, its seat, its premises or the number of members of the college of decision will take place without an opinion, without the knowledge of the host of the province concerned. The opinion of the voivodship sejm cannot be replaced, ladies and gentlemen, by the central opinion of the committee of common government and self-government. Organisations, associations of different levels of government have a negative opinion on this project. I didn't get a single positive opinion from the local government. Particular opposition has been raised by the determination that there is over-indebtedness in the local government sector. That is not true. Local governments only admit individual, accidental cases of over-indebtedness of local government units. In conclusion, this project is a direct impact on the constitutionally guaranteed autonomy of local government units, and only the perversely justified by the alleged concern for the local government and its economic position. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u442	Thank you, Minister. The discussion is closed. A motion to reject the draft law at first reading was put forward in the discussion. We proceed with the examination of item 20 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the law on micro-organisms and genetically modified organisms and some other laws (print No 1424). I ask the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Environment of Mr Andrzej Szweda-Lewandowski to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u443	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the law on micro-organisms and genetically modified organisms regulates the cultivation of genetically modified crops in particular to the following extent: notification to the competent Polish authorities of GMO crops and the notification of their location in accordance with Part C of Directive 2001/18 on the deliberate release into the environment of genetically modified organisms - the authority of the act provided for in this act is the Minister of the Environment; the way in which information on the location of GMO crops is made public in the so-called. the GMO crop register - it is also maintained by the Ministry of the Environment; crop control regarding the detection of genetic modification - and here the control body according to the provisions of the Act is the State Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspection. The aim of the Act is to increase the level of safety of human health and the environment by establishing mechanisms for effective control and possible reduction of GMO crops, complementing the implementation of the provisions of Directive 2001/18 of the European Parliament and of the Council and implementing the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union in Case C-78/13, which concerned the absence of provisions governing the notification and registration of GMO crops and the notification of them to the public in the Polish legal order. At present, there is no cultivation of GMOs in Poland, and this is possible because in March 2015 a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council 2015/412 has entered into force, which amended the original Directive 2001/18 to allow Member States to restrict or prohibit the cultivation of genetically modified organisms in their territory. And this new legislation gives Member States the freedom to decide whether they want to grow GMOs in their territory without changing the risk assessment procedure provided for in the EU GMO authorisation system or not. The Republic of Poland took advantage of the possibility offered by the revision of this Directive and in September 2015 it announced its intention to exclude its territory from the cultivation of eight types of modified maize which are currently authorised for cultivation in the European Union. The possible cultivation of GMOs, according to this amendment of the Act, will require the establishment of a GMO cultivation zone by the farmer in which the cultivation can be carried out. The zone may be established after obtaining an authorisation issued by the Minister responsible for the environment - issued again after consulting the Minister responsible for Agriculture and the opinion of the municipality council where the crop is planned. An important element of the evaluation of the proposal will be its potential impact on environmental safety and pollinating insects, in particular bees, of course. Prior to the creation of the zone, it will also be necessary to obtain written statements from owners and users of perpetual real estate, as well as beekeepers or beekeepers' associations whose real estate or apiaries are located up to 3 km from the agricultural land where the cultivation of GMOs is planned, that they do not object to the intention to establish such a zone. Control of GMO crops is a task that has not yet been carried out in Poland on such a large scale. The competent authority carrying out the checks shall be the State Inspection of Plant Protection and Seed Protection. This inspection shall have experience in carrying out checks on the use of seed in the detection of genetically modified varieties, inter alia. corn. Crop control will include field control in 16 voivodships, substantive supervision and coordination of tasks in voivodships, including proceedings on administrative decisions, i.e. imposition of fines. The testing will be carried out at the Central Laboratory of the Central Plant Protection and Seed Protection Inspectorate in Toruń. The entire control tasks will be supervised by the Chief Plant Protection and Seed Inspector. The control will be subject to cultivation on the basis of a possible authorisation to establish a GMO cultivation zone as well as other crops which are reasonably suspected to be GMO crops. The checks will cover both documents, e.g. verification of evidence of purchase of seed and sampling directly from the field. The Act also provides for criminal sanctions, namely the cultivation of GMOs without the required authorisation or in breach of the conditions and methods laid down in the authorisation to establish a GMO cultivation zone will be punishable by an administrative penalty of 10 000. PLN up to 30,000. PLN for each hectare of cultivation started. The same monetary penalty will apply to those who farm non-commercial GMOs for cultivation. At present, in the current state of law, when there is a ban on cultivation of GMOs in Poland, this prohibition means that even after the introduction of the abovementioned. products will not be allowed to be placed on the market by the relevant EU regulations in Poland. Any application for the establishment of an area designated for cultivation of GMOs for these products will not be considered and no authorisation will be granted for the establishment of a zone. Similarly, where an application for authorisation for the establishment of a GMO cultivation zone will concern GMO plants which have not yet been placed on the market for cultivation in the European Union. Thank you very much.

During the 2010 conference, the U.S. economic society reported that wildly growing transgenic rape was found in the United States. Scientists have admitted that there is an uncontrollable spread of GMO plants on a large scale in the United States. Previously genetically modified plants were found in Canada, Japan and the United Kingdom. According to the 2006 GreenWatch and Greenpeace International report, there have been cases of illegal GMO cultivation, release into the environment and mixing with unmodified plants, including wild plants, in 39 countries. Since other countries have negative experiences and despite very good surveillance mechanisms cannot control the release of GMOs into the environment, it would be naive to think that in Poland we will avoid similar contamination. As Poland, we can boast of good quality food for which we do not use hectolitres of plant protection products. With these advantages we should make a key element in promoting our food outside Poland. We cannot put our farmers at risk of accidental contamination resulting in their crops and income. We cannot allow today's thriving organic food market to be destroyed. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u453	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a position on printing No. 1424, a government bill amending the law on micro-organisms and genetically modified organisms and some other laws. The proposed amendment is the result of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union C 478/13, whose implementation was based on the establishment of a statutory obligation to notify the competent Polish authorities of the location of GMO crops in accordance with Directive 18 on the deliberate release into the environment of modified organisms. In addition, as is apparent from the judgment, it was necessary to establish a register of the location of these crops and to make publicly available the location of GMO crops. Strong resistance to genetically modified plants remains in the European Union. Poland belongs to Member States that do not agree to the cultivation of such plants on their territory. The amendment is correct, although in practice it does not make significant changes for consumers in Poland. As early as January 2013, Poland introduced a ban on cultivation of GMOs authorised in the European Union, namely MON 810 maize and Amflora potato, under the Government Regulation, justifying this decision that genetically modified plants cannot be grown without the risk of contamination of natural varieties. The next step with a far greater impact on the life of consumers, but also the breeders, is to prohibit the production, placing on the market and use in animal feed of GMOs containing shoots of genetically modified oil plants which are expected to enter into force in 2019. The government has recently committed itself to decisive measures to support the denial of GMO feed from the home market and to strengthen the commodity production of the domestic protein product. At what stage are these activities? In order to complete the regulations aimed at eliminating GMO cultivation in Poland, it is necessary to implement the safety clause with Article. 23 Directive 2001/18, which allows bans on different types of GMOs rather than collective bans. She did so, for example. Austria, which, pursuant to the above mentioned paragraph, the clause introduced a ban on the marketing of transgenic maize MON 810 seed material, which is the only variety authorised for cultivation in the European Union. According to Art. Article 49a (1) (a) (ii) (ii) (ii) (ii) (iii) 3 The establishment of a zone designated for cultivation of GMOs will require the fulfilment of many difficult conditions, as well as the authorisation of the Minister responsible for the environment and the consent of the Minister for Agriculture and the municipality in which the cultivation of GMOs is planned. In practice, legal cultivation of GMOs will not be viable. The project contains minor inaccuracies. It has not been specified on what basis the documentation on the impact on environmental safety to be provided by a farmer wishing to start cultivation of GMOs is to be based on. Long-term studies on the effects of GMO introduction on the environment and the food chain are still lacking. There is also no consensus among scientists on the harm done to genetically modified plants themselves. However, it has been proven that GMO cultivation leads to the loss of biodiversity and increases the use of chemical plant protection products, thereby endangering traditional and organic crops. The advantage of Polish agriculture is biodiversity, very high biological and health quality of products, high export potential, therefore it is necessary to ensure the competitive position of Polish food in the European Union. At the same time, I must stress that we do not oppose the use of GMOs in pharmacy. In view of the above, the Posel Club Modern is ready to support the proposed project. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u477	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I have the honour to present a report on the parliamentary draft resolution on commemorating the 140th anniversary of the birth of Bolesław Biadas, print No 1443. Bolesław Bięgas, or actually Bolesław Biębialski, was born on 29 March 1877 in a small village of Koziczyn near Ciechanow in a poor peasant family. Early orphaned, he spent his childhood in the countryside, shepherding cattle and sticking clay figurines. His artistic talent was initially noticed by the local priest. Aleksandra Rzewnicki, who with the financial support of the parishioners sent young Poles to the Warsaw sculptor Antoni Panasiuk. His next protector was the Ciechanian doctor and art patron Franciszek Rajkowski, who presented early attempts at sculpting his subject. Adam Krasiński from Opinogóra was the grandson of the poet Zygmunt Krasiński, and Alexander Świętochowsky. Thanks to the public subscription announced by Świętochowski, funds were collected so that the young artist could receive education at the Krakow School of Fine Arts, where he started his studies under the direction of Alfred Daun and Konstanty Laszczka. In 1901, he was removed from the university in the atmosphere of scandal for making a sculpture of the book of life, which violated the canons of art at that time. Foreign artistic criticism was more favorable to his works, as indicated by his excellent reviews during his participation in the X Exhibition of the Vienna Secession, which took place in 1901. In subsequent editions of this exhibition he participated in the following years. He also presented his sculptures in the Warsaw salon of Alexander Krywult, also in the gallery of the Society for the Encouragement of Fine Arts in Warsaw. Thanks to the scholarship of the Warsaw Association of Encouragements of Fine Arts, he went to Paris, where he stayed for the rest of his life. In Paris, he began to study at the high school of fine arts, but quickly abandoned it, he took up his own work financially supported by new patrons, baronosty of Henryk and Jadwiga Trüschelów. He quickly gained recognition in the circle of French symbolists, and his works were exhibited in numerous views of the Independent Salon, the Salon of the National Association of Fine Arts and the Autumn Salon. He died on September 30, 1954, and was buried in the cemetery of Polish emigration in Montmorency in the tomb of the Historical and Literary Society, whose member he became at the end of his life. Bolesław Bięgas, one of the most respected artists in Europe, is still very little known in Poland. His artistic achievements consist of sculptures, paintings, as well as 16 dramas, several lectures on art and novels and poems. Bolesław Bięgas is considered the leading representative of the symbolic and secessional direction not only in Polish, but also in European sculpture. He managed to preserve his individual form of work, which is still astonishing by its unique diversity and richness of subject matter. The fame brought the porter bust to Runas, and he was successful in taking on themes inspired by music and literature. Poles' sculptural work was subject to, among others. influences of Stanisław Przybyszewski's philosophy. Inspired by his works, he created allegorical sculptures that address the issues of human existence. At the same time he created sculptures of synthetic, geometrized form, subject to fascination with Prasłowian cuisine. One of the inspirations was the Worldhide. He created works about precursors e.g. The already mentioned book of life preceded the synthetic compositions of Xawery Dunikowski, while the works of the Earth, Love of Death, and the enchanted Palaces realized visions which only 20 years later were born in the imagination of surrealists. After World War I, the sculpture activity of the Poles weakened for the sake of painting. A series of dozens of paintings by war vampires from 1914 to 1918 draws attention from the rich art of painting. Painting started earlier, about 1900, initially inspired by Wyspiański's works. Later he developed his distinctive style in: a series of spherical portraits, in which human faces intersect with a grid of lines and planes divided by arcs and geometric shapes; in satirical political paintings, among others. Russian-Japanese War, Nero on Polish soil; also in a series of paintings depicting stylized mythological figures of titanide and titans with superhuman shapes and strength and in many other paintings. The author also wrote literary works, poems, which were maintained in the symbolic convention. He also wrote drama, as I mentioned. It is worth noting that Bolesław Bięgas enjoyed the recognition of the contemporary authorities of the art world. The Renaissance of his work has lasted since the 1970s. 20th century French and Polish art historians organize numerous exhibitions of symbolists, where painting and sculpting works of the Poles are presented. The paintings and sculptures of the Poles can be found, among others. in the Orsay Museum in Paris, the Museum of Fine Arts in Lyon, the Royal Museum of Fine Arts in Brussels, the Polish Museum in Chicago, the Museum of the Poles in Paris, the Museum of Mazowiecki in Płock, the National Museum in Warsaw and Poznan, and the Museum of the Mazowiecka Nobility in Ciechanów. In recent years the artist's family land has made efforts to commemorate this great artist. In the District Centre for Culture and Art. Maria Konopnicka in Ciechanów was opened the world's first gallery of Bolesław Bięgas, and on the facade of her building was exposed a mural inspired by the image of Bolesław Bięgas. Also in Warsaw the Foundation im. Bolesława Bięgas, thanks to which on 6 May 2015 in the capital was opened the largest permanent exhibition in our country of painting and sculpture of this outstanding Polish artist. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering the draft resolution at its meeting on 5 April this year, asks the High Sejm to adopt this draft resolution as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the 140th anniversary of the birth of Bolesław Bięgas. On 29 March 2017, the 140th anniversary of the birth of Bolesław Biegłas (in fact: Bolesław Biegnalski) - one of the most original Polish artists at the turn of the 19th and 20th century, a sculptor and symbolist painter, whose work continues to impress with its unique diversity and richness of subjects. Bolesław Bięgas (1877-1954), one of the most respected artists in Europe, is still very little known in Poland. His artistic achievements, which consist of sculptures, paintings, as well as 16 dramas and several lectures on art, did not finally reach the Vistula, and therefore the Runas did not exist in the national consciousness. There are several reasons for this: the artist settled permanently in Paris in 1901, criticizing his works in his homeland, death during communism, which was completely rejected by the Runas system, deciding to entrust his artistic legacy to Polish emigration in France. After 1989, the works of Bolesław Bięgas remained unknown in Poland, although in the world of his works they have been returning to art salons for half a century. The trial of time survived not only his symbolic sculptures of precursor qualities, differing from the fashionable contemporary art style, but also intriguing painting works, both unique symbolic compositions, full of hidden meanings and portraits, including a series of painting spherical portraits. His works can be seen in many museums, among others. in the Orsay Museum in Paris, the Museum of Fine Arts in Lyon, the National Museums in Warsaw, Gdansk, Poznań and in more and more private and Polish collections. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland honours the memory of Bolesław Biadas and praises his artistic achievements. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u479	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Bolesław Biegnalski was born on 29 March 1877 in Koziczyn village near Ciechanów in a poor noble family. Even as a child, he was sticking clay figures and sculpting shepherd's sticks. In 1886, he had a painful experience of his father and younger brother's death, and his mother died four years later. Interestingly, Biędzyski's sculpture talent was noticed by the local parish priest, and thanks to his support he was able to learn lessons from Warsaw's sculptor Antoni Panasiuk. Bolesław fell ill because of ill treatment and returned home from Warsaw. In Ciechanów, a young sculptor saw Alexander Świętochowski, who was amazed by the work of an orphaned artist, and thus convinced him of the genius of the young artist. And that's when he changed his name to Runas for the young Biker so that he could promote it as an authentic product of folk culture. In September 1896, in Wendy's bookstore in Warsaw, Świętochowski organized the first exhibition of sculptures. Thanks to the support and help of his friends, Runas started his studies at the School of Fine Arts in Krakow. In his studies he began to achieve his first artistic success. In 1898 he received a bronze medal for the work of the Ganimeda kidnap, and a year later he exhibited seven sculptures in Krywult's living room. Thanks to his participation in the prestigious Secession Exhibition in Vienna, he became a member of the Exhibition and exhibited it several times more. In 1901 he presented his works in the Society of Fine Arts in Warsaw. For the sculpture of the book of life, Runas was removed from his studies in the atmosphere of scandal. He left for Paris, where he stayed for the rest of his life. He was a member of the Polish Historical and Literary Society in Paris. Under the influence of Stanisław Przybyszewski's philosophy, he created sculptures about the subject of the drama of human existence. For the stylistics of his works, dynamic forms with flaming outline and expressive deformed faces-masks are significant. The artist also wrote literary works, poems, novels, and dramatic works maintained in the symbolic convention. At Stanisław Wyspiański's suggestion, he also started painting. In the early '30s. In the 20th century, the figure of Runas began to fade into oblivion. The artist fell into solitude. The cultural achievements of Bolesław Runas all over the world still function today, and his works often [[♪ The bell ♪]] are more recognizable abroad than in Poland. In order to save the artist from oblivion, on 6 May 2015 in Warsaw the largest permanent exhibition of painting and sculpture of Bolesław Biedzas was opened, and the Sejm of the Republic of Poland adopted a resolution commemorating its creator. The Law and Justice Club supports the draft resolution and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Recovering independence in 1918 was also due to a multitude of anonymous Poles, both civilians and soldiers. The individual experience of millions of Poles cannot be forgotten. Among them, in addition to editorial amendments, there are also substantive amendments. One of the revised solutions is mediation, which, as a new institution under administrative law, is intended to provide an alternative to the way in which cases are dealt with so far. The first amendment of the Senate concerns the limitation of the possibility of selecting a mediator in these proceedings to permanent lists of mediators and letters led by non-governmental organisations and universities which may create mediation centres. This is a provision ensuring that mediation is conducted by people who are appropriately qualified and with appropriate experience. I have no doubt that this change, by guaranteeing the competence and competence of the mediator, will benefit all parties involved in this process. The next amendment introduced by the Senate concerns the repeal of § This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. 163c, according to which, in the case of simplified proceedings carried out on the basis of an official form submitted, new circumstances could be notified only at a time when they would be relevant and at the same time not be extended. I agree with the argument that the rights of the parties cannot be restricted simply because this may extend the procedure. This confusion of priorities and it is clear to me that this cannot be the case. It is also clear that if the deadline can be postponed, it is enough to pass on to the general principles of the Code of Administrative Procedure and the problem of the deadline will cease to be a problem. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u524	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club to present my position on the Senate resolution on the Act amending the excise duty Act. First of all, it should be recalled that European Union law provides for the exemption from excise duty of wholly contaminated alcohol. However, certain contaminating measures, including in particular the Hungarian contaminater, are unsustainable or easy to remove, which facilitates tax fraud. Unfortunately, in Poland there are many such frauds. At the same time, the courts allowed us to interpret that ethyl alcohol can be contaminated in any way permitted in any Member State, including the Hungarian method. The regulation contained in the bill is necessary and deserves a positive assessment. It removes the question of interpretation that has been raised against the background of the existing wording. As a consequence, new solutions cannot be exempted from excise duty produced in Poland by alcohol completely contaminated according to the Hungarian method. The Senate amendment provides for the introduction of a mandatory register of operators receiving completely contaminated alcohol and certain products containing ethyl alcohol. The register is intended to allow monitoring of the use of completely contaminated alcohol and the products I mentioned. It is to be run by sales entities, namely sales in packs of 20 litres or more. The amendment deserves a positive assessment. It is doubtful to refer only to the Polish Classification of Products and Services, not to the Combined Nomenclature, CN, and as we know from the PKWiU already in this sphere we have actually left. Overall, however, the assessment is positive. Thank you very much.

April 4th this year In the Art Collections in Włocławek the post-competition exhibition was officially opened with awards to the winners of the 9th edition of the sculpture competition. Stanisław Zagajewski. The competition from three editions has a nationwide range and is increasingly attracted by young sculptors who are inspired by the work of Włocławski's artist. This is demonstrated by the number of nearly 200 participants, including 70 of schools and artistic centres from all over the country. For years, the project has aroused admiration and respect for the organizers, as well as participants who keep their memory so beautifully from the late prominent Italian artist Stanisław Zagajewski and at the same time pursue their artistic passion. Stanisław Zagajewski was born around 1927 and died on April 4, 2008 in Włocławek. Polish sculptor, self-educed, raw art artist, intuitive artist, referred to as Włocławski Nikifora. The date and place of his birthday are unknown. He was found in winter on the steps of St. Barbary in Warsaw. The child was assigned a name and entered a fictional date and place of birth. He spent his childhood in a nun's nursing home. He made a living doing a lot of different jobs. Arts of firing clay forms taught him in the cooperation of the work of Sztuka Kujawska in Włocławek. The complete collection - a permanent exhibition of more than 120 sculptures, including several altars, is owned by the Kujawska and Dobrzyńska Museum in Włocławek. Zagajewski's sculptures also have in their collection, among others. museums in Warsaw, Katowice, Toruń and Lausanne. The artist was awarded the Bronze Medal of Gloria Artis, the special award of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage and the Honorary Medal of the Senate of Poland. Lonely by choice. He once said that talent is a great thing, it's a mystery, it's a great power in a man. A real artist equals a wild animal. The content of these few words has become the essence of his humble life. Sacrificing himself completely for art and giving up entirely on temporal goods, he devoted himself to the passion of creation. He lived art and art. He locked his artistic visions in unique works, their fame has an international dimension. Another edition of the sculpture competition. Stanisław Zagajewski shows how real today the dream of Włocławski Nikifor's memory of his works and the upbringing of successors became. In the Museum of Kujawska and Dobrzyńska Land and the Brewery B Culture Center, which was run by seven editions, I participated as the guardian of the students of the competition. I am proud of the fact that also in this year's edition my former pupils became finalists of the competition. I am delighted that as a Member of the Polish Parliament, I was also able to fund a special prize in the competition. Congratulations to all the winners and participants. I would like to express my special thanks to the students' tutors for the great work they have put into their preparation, for raising the interest in art in young people. I wish everyone further artistic successes, broadening their horizons and continuing search for creative solutions, organizers - many successful editions of this prestigious and important for us, Włocławian, competition. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u556	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 24 and 25 March in Konin the 21st National Football tournament was held. Olka Ruminkiewicz about the Cup of the President of the Republic of Andrzej Duda. At first glance one can say - a tournament like many other tournaments in Poland organized for children up to the age of 10 by those who are at heart of passing on the youngest in a sporty way the right relationship between people so as to enjoy it at the same time. However, going into the history of this tournament and reaching its origins, we will note that the initiative to create the first indoor football competitions for children in Konin was created as a result of very tragic events in 1996. Let me say a few words about this. One day after dark, ten-year-old Olek Ruminkiewicz leaves for the neighborhood arcade. It's a trailer where a couple of gold kids can play fliers. His father gives him money without suspecting he's seeing his son for the last time, because his son's not coming home. The search begins. Two days later, Father Wojciech Ruminkiewicz receives a call stating that Olek is alive, is safe, and there is a ransom demand. Even though his father managed to raise the amount of money he wanted with his family and friends, he did not save his son. A 10-year-old boy Olek Ruminkiewicz was murdered and thrown into a well like a worthless and unnecessary thing. That's how Olek's life stopped, the baby, as many others did. The time stopped, the lack of dreaming, the lack of joy and sorrow, the lack of laughing faces of innocent children. Tragism of a global dimension adds that the murderer was a judge who previously judged criminals by law. The world has also collapsed for parents who are difficult to accept victims. In a moment, someone brutally ruined their lives by ruining their family. Parents also had no more dreams and hopes of their children being taken away by death. What parents feel about murdered children can only be understood by those who have experienced similar tragic events. Often families can't get up from their knees and suffer their whole lives. Fortunately, there are those who, constantly feeling pain in their hearts after the loss of their beloved child, try to continue to stop in time, not realized by their sons and daughters' dreams. To them, among others. Wojciech Ruminkiewicz has been in possession of 700 PLN of pension for several years. Immediately after his son was murdered, he decided to establish a nationwide association of families, which unfortunately have similar tragic experiences. He knew that in this group he would find motivation, strength and sense to work with children. Therefore, every year at one of the primary schools in Konin organizes a children's football tournament - Olek's memory. The main idea of the tournament is to commemorate Olek Ruminkiewicz and other children who became victims of violence, who, like all the participants of the tournament, could, competing with each other, enjoy as much as possible, develop their personality. Today they certainly patronize every competition in Konin. So it is good that the presidents of the Republic of Poland joined this patronage, from Lech Kaczyński to Andrzej Duda, who took over the 21st tournament with his patronage two weeks ago. Various politicians come to the competition, including Members, offering interesting prizes and cups, as well as Polish artists. At the last tournament, young athletes and their group were accompanied by Don Vasyl. Kids play soccer for two days and play. They're not missing anything. All this is provided by tournament organizers and team maintainers competing with each other. Looking aside at the involvement of many people in organizing this tournament, at the sincere joy of the players and their rivalry, you can confidently congratulate you today, Wojtek, on the idea. Almost from the beginning I have been trying to support you in this undertaking, because I believe that we must not waste time for children. Polish football tournament Children. Olka Ruminkiewicz in Konin is your success. Without him, boys from the Public Primary School No. 6 from Radomska could not enjoy this year, who won this prestigious tournament and won many valuable prizes, including the President Andrzej Duda Cup and prizes funded by Prime Minister Beata Szydło, President of the Polish Football Association Zbigniew Bońek and the Mayor's Cup of Konin Józef Nowicki. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you, Wojtek, for what you are doing, and I wish you perseverance in passing on the positive energy that is so much needed, especially for people with similar experiences to you. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-06-sejm-39-2.u558	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On April 2, we celebrated World Autism Awareness Day. Autism is a disease that affects more and more people. Statistics in this respect are alarming. The number of autisms has increased six times in the last 20 years. By the time I finish my speech, there will be an estimated 250 children in the world. Most likely, two of them will be diagnosed with overall developmental disorders, including autism. The causes of this disorder are not yet fully known. Doctors believe that autism is caused by abnormal brain function in early childhood. This can be influenced, among others. genetic factors, pregnancy and childbirth disorders. Autism is a developmental disorder that affects how a person affected by a disease perceives the world, how he interacts with the environment and how he processes information from the environment. Autistics usually find it difficult to communicate with other people, have problems with communication, abstract thinking, or with learning. According to estimates, 5 million people live with autism in Europe, for which the change in public awareness of their illness is truly fundamental. According to the Charter on the Rights of Autism adopted by the European Parliament, people with autism have a number of rights. Nevertheless, many people affected by this condition still do not have the possibility to use specialist diagnostics, therapy, education on a daily basis. Many of these people face a number of barriers in the labour market, have problems settling in the nearest environment and face many, often prosaic problems of everyday life. Remember how important it is not only once a year to show that we are open to people with autism and their problems. Let's not be green about autism. Not only in April, but let's be blue all year round. It is the color of solidarity with people with autism and their families. In my parliamentary work, I met people with autism, their loved ones, their families, and these are special people. I'm convinced that even though I tried to help them, I actually got more than they got from me. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
